The proof of (i) follows immediately chasing this diagram.

We next prove (ii). In the exact sequence

(5.9)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} 0\longrightarrow\,{\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\xrightarrow{\,\delta\,}&\,{\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\ker\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\right)\longrightarrow 0\,,\end{aligned}

the left-most module is torsion-free, and hence the induced map

\delta_{\rm tf}\colon\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tf}\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}

is still injective. Moreover, both
{\rm coker}(\delta_{\rm tf})
and
{\rm coker}(\delta)
are
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-torsion modules, and we have

(5.10)

0\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\longrightarrow{\rm coker}(\delta)\longrightarrow{\rm coker}(\delta_{\rm tf})\longrightarrow 0\,.

It follows from Lemma
5.14
and (
5.10
) that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}&\left({\rm coker}(\delta)\right)\,\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\\
&=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tf}\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\delta^{(2)}\left(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\right)}\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\right).\end{aligned}

This, combined with (
5.9
) and Theorem
4.3
(ii) yields

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tf}\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\delta^{(2)}\left(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\right)}\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\right)\,,\end{aligned}

and Part (iii) (which implicitly asserts that
\delta^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\subset{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
) follows since we have
{\bigcap}^{r}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}X={\bigcap}^{r}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}X_{\rm tf}
(by the definition of exterior bi-duals).
∎

Let us set
Z:=\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})}{\mathrm{res}_{p}(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset}))}
to ease notation. The map

\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}}Z\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}

(see Lemma
5.16
(ii)) is injective, the source is torsion-free, and both the source and target are of rank
2
. By Lemma
5.14
,
\mathrm{res}_{p}
induces an injective map

{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}}{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z_{\rm tf}\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}={\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,,

allowing us to define the submodule
\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}\left(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\right)
of
{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z\otimes_{\varpi_{3,2}^{*}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
.

Lemma 5.17
.

Suppose that (

nV
T

a

)
) holds. Then:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\,&\left(\dfrac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\,\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(Z_{\rm tor}\right)\\
&\,=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\delta^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\right)\,\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\right).\end{aligned}

Proof.

Consider the exact sequence

(5.12)

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow Z\xrightarrow{\,\,\partial\,\,}

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\longrightarrow H^{2}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})=0\,.

It induces an injective map
\partial_{\rm tf}\colon Z_{\rm tf}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tf}
of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
-modules of rank 2, and Lemma
5.14
gives rise to a map

\partial^{(2)}\colon{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z={\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z_{\rm tf}\longrightarrow{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tf}={\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\,,

which has the following properties.

The diagram

\textstyle{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}}
\scriptstyle{\delta^{(2)}}
\textstyle{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}
\textstyle{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial^{(2)}}

commutes.

We have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}&\left({\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,\big/\,\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left({\rm coker}(\partial_{\rm tf})\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left({\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,\big/\,\partial^{(2)}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left({\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,\big/\,\delta^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\right)\,,\end{aligned}

where the second equality follows from property (a) above.

We note further, thanks to (
5.12
), that the sequence

(5.13)

0\longrightarrow Z_{\rm tor}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\longrightarrow{\rm coker}(\partial)\longrightarrow{\rm coker}(\partial_{\rm tf})\longrightarrow 0

is exact. Combining (
5.13
) with property (b), we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\,&\left(\dfrac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\,\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(Z_{\rm tor}\right)\\
&\,=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\delta^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\right)\,\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{\rm tor}\right).\end{aligned}

as required.
∎

Corollary 5.18
.

In the setting of Lemma
5.17
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\,&\left(\dfrac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\right)\varpi_{3,2}^{*}\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)\,\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(Z_{\rm tor}\right).\end{aligned}

The proof of the following lemma is identical to that of Lemma
5.5
.

Lemma 5.19
.

Assume that (

nV
T

a

)
), (

AJ
p

¯
Π

)
) and (

P
-

¯
Π

)
) hold. Then:

\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})=\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\,.

5.2.3.

In preparation for our main result, we will prove Lemma
5.20
below. Let us fix a height-one prime
\mathfrak{q}
of
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
. For notational simplicity, we put

\displaystyle S:={\mathcal{R}}_{3,\mathfrak{p}},\quad W_{i}:=H^{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p},\mathscr{F}^{i}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}S,\quad N_{?}

\displaystyle:=\textrm{the image of }\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{?})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}S\textrm{ in }W_{0},

\displaystyle L_{?}

\displaystyle:=\textrm{the image of }\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{?}})\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}S\textrm{ in }W_{0}.

Note that
Z\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}S=W_{0}/L_{0}
and
Z_{\rm tor}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}S=(W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tor}
.

Lemma 5.20
.

We have

\Char_{S}(\bigwedge^{2}(W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tf}/\bigwedge^{2}N_{a})\Char_{S}((W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tors})=\Char_{S}(W_{0}/(L_{0}+N_{a})).

Proof.

One can choose a basis
\{e_{1},e_{2},e_{3},e_{4}\}
of
W_{0}
such that
L_{0}=Sae_{1}+Sbe_{2}
. Let us put

N_{a}=S(a_{1}e_{1}+b_{1}e_{2}+c_{1}e_{3}+d_{1}e_{4})+S(a_{2}e_{1}+b_{2}e_{2}+c_{2}e_{3}+d_{2}e_{4})\,.

Since
(W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tors}=W_{0}/(Se_{1}+Se_{2})
, we have

\Char_{S}\left(\bigwedge^{2}(W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tf}\,\Big/\,\bigwedge^{2}N_{a}\right)=S(c_{1}d_{2}-c_{2}d_{1}).

Hence,

\Char_{S}\left(\bigwedge^{2}(W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tf}\big/\bigwedge^{2}N_{a}\right)\cdot\Char_{S}\left((W_{0}/L_{0})_{\rm tors}\right)=S(ab(c_{1}d_{2}-c_{2}d_{1})).

The proof of our lemma follows since we also have

\displaystyle\Char_{S}\left(W_{0}\Big/(L_{0}+N_{a})\right)=S\det\begin{pmatrix}a&0&0&0\\
0&b&0&0\\
a_{1}&b_{1}&c_{1}&d_{1}\\
a_{2}&b_{2}&c_{2}&d_{2}\end{pmatrix}=S(ab(c_{1}d_{2}-c_{2}d_{1})).

∎

Lemma 5.21
.

Assume that (

nV
T

a

)
) holds. We have,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})&=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\,\oplus\,\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}{\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}\right)\,.\end{aligned}

Proof.

The exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}}\dfrac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))}\longrightarrow\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\longrightarrow 0

combined with Theorem
4.3
(ii), Corollary
5.18
, and Lemma
5.20
shows that the left side of (
5.14
) can be written as

(5.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}Z\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)&\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(Z_{\rm tor}\right)\\
&\hskip-71.13188pt\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}{\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}\right)\\
&\hskip-142.26378pt=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\,\oplus\,\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\\
&\hskip-71.13188pt\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}{\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}\right)\,.\end{aligned}

∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Assume that (

nV
T

a

)
) holds.
The following canonical homomorphism is an isomorphism:

\displaystyle{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}

\displaystyle\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\,\otimes\,{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\xrightarrow{\,\,\sim\,\,}{\bigcap}^{4}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\,\oplus\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right).

Proof.

This lemma follows from both
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-modules
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})
are of rank 2 under our running hypothesis.
∎

5.2.4.

The restriction map

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\oplus\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}}H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})

\displaystyle(x,y)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathrm{res}_{p}(x)+\mathrm{res}_{p}(y)

where
\mathrm{res}_{p}(x)
is defined using the middle vertical arrow in (
5.8
), together with Lemma
5.22
induce a homomorphism

\displaystyle\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}\,\colon\,{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}

\displaystyle\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\,\otimes\,{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle(\hbox{Lemma}~\ref{lemma_2025_07_10_1431})\qquad

\displaystyle\qquad\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}\,{\bigcap}^{4}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\oplus\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 162.18062pt\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{\otimes 4}}{\bigcap}^{4}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})\cong{\mathcal{R}}_{3},

where the final isomorphism amounts to choosing a basis of the cyclic
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
-module
{\bigcap}^{4}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}H^{1}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
.

Proposition 5.23
.

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))
.

Proof.

If (

nV
T

a

)
) fails while both
\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})
and
\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})
are non-zero, then Lemma
5.8
implies that the homomorphism
\mathrm{res}_{p}^{\otimes 4}
vanishes.
Consequently,
\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}\big(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})\big)=0
.
Thus, we may assume without loss of generality that (

nV
T

a

)
) holds.

Both
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-modules
{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})
and
{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})
are torsion-free of rank one under (

nV
T

a

)
). Hence,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}{\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}\right)\\
&\qquad\qquad=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}{\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})}\right).\end{aligned}

Moreover, observe that we have

(5.17)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}(\coker(\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}))=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\,\oplus\,\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})}\right)\end{aligned}

The proof of our proposition follows on combining (
5.14
), (
5.16
) with (
5.17
) and Lemma
5.14
.
∎

Remark 5.24
.

In this remark, we briefly explain that an analogue (
5.18
) of Proposition
5.23
holds in the situation of §
5.1
as well, and Theorem
5.1
can be deduced from that.

Suppose that
\delta(T_{3}^{{\dagger}},\Delta_{d})\neq 0=\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
. By Lemma
5.3
and Lemma
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{d})=

\displaystyle\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\dfrac{H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}{\mathrm{res}_{p}(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}}))\oplus\mathrm{res}_{p}(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d}))}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d}))}{\delta(M_{3}^{{\dagger}},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})}\right)\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})}{\delta(T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})}\right).

The same argument as in the proof of Proposition
5.23
shows that

(5.18)

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{d})=\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3}}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})).

Here,
\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3}}^{(2)}
is given as the compositum of the morphisms

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})

\displaystyle\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\,\sim\,}{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})\oplus\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})\right)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}_{p}^{(2)}}{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3})\simeq{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,.

As before, let us denote by
\mathrm{Log}_{d/e}
the composite map

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})\oplus\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{res}_{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\cong{\mathcal{R}}_{3}

and
\mathrm{Log}_{c}
as the composite

\displaystyle\ker(\mathrm{Log}_{d/e})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{res}_{p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2}/\mathscr{F}^{3})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\cong{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,.

Then, we have by definition

\mathrm{Log}^{(2)}_{\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3}}=\mathrm{Log}_{d/e}\wedge\mathrm{Log}_{c}.

Since
\mathrm{Log}_{d/e}(\delta(T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}}))=0
by Lemma
5.5
, we obtain

\displaystyle\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{d})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3}}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}}))

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Log}_{d/e}\wedge\mathrm{Log}_{c})(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{d})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Log}_{d/e}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{d}))\,\cdot\,\mathrm{Log}_{c}(\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}}))\,,

concluding this alternative proof of Theorem
5.1
.

\blacksquare

5.2.5.

Our proof of Theorem
5.13
will mimic the second proof of Theorem
5.1
we have recorded in Remark
5.24
. To execute this plan, we will show (cf. Lemma
5.27
below) that the saturations
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
and
H^{1}_{M}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
of the isomorphic images of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})
inside
H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
, respectively, have the following property: The quotient
H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{?}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
is a free
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-module of rank two for each
?\in\{{\underline{\Pi}},M\}
.

We will need the following general facts (recorded as Lemma
5.25
and Lemma
5.26
below) in our proof of this property.

Lemma 5.25
.

A reflexive module of rank one over a regular local ring
S
is free.

Proof.

Let
I
be a reflexive ideal of
S
, and let
\mathfrak{p}
be a height-one prime of
S
. Since regular local rings are unique factorisation domains (by a well-known theorem of Auslander–Buchsbaum), the height-one prime
\mathfrak{p}
is principal. Let
x_{\mathfrak{p}}
be a generator of
\mathfrak{p}
, and let
n_{\mathfrak{p}}
be the non-negative integer such that
IS_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathfrak{p}^{n_{\mathfrak{p}}}S_{\mathfrak{p}}
. We put
x:=\prod_{\mathrm{ht}\,{\mathfrak{p}}=1}x_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n_{\mathfrak{p}}}\in S
. Then
IS_{\mathfrak{p}}=xS_{\mathfrak{p}}
for any height-one prime
\mathfrak{q}
of
S
. Since
I
is reflexive, this implies
I=\bigcap_{\mathrm{ht}\,{\mathfrak{p}}=1}xS_{\mathfrak{p}}=xS
(cf.
[
Sak23
]
, Lemma C.11).
∎

Lemma 5.26
.

Let
S
be a regular local ring.
For any finitely generated torsion-free
S
-module
M
, we have

\mathrm{Ext}_{S}^{1}(M,R)=0.

Proof.

This is well-known. See, for example,
[
Nek06
, §9.1.3(v) and (vi)]
.
∎

Lemma 5.27
.

Assume that Hypotheses
5.2.1
hold.
Then both
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-modules

H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})\quad\hbox{ and }\quad H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{M}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})

are free of rank 2.

Remark 5.28
.

In the situation of §
5.1
, the saturation of
\mathrm{res}_{p}\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})
in
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{3})

equals
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2}/\mathscr{F}^{3})
.
Therefore, the analogous quotient in this case is isomorphic to
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{1}/\mathscr{F}^{2})
, which is free of rank one.

\blacksquare

Proof of Lemma
5.27
.

Let us show that
H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
is free. For
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, we let
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{i}})
denote the saturation of the isomorphic image of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{i}})
inside
H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
.
Then it suffices to show that

H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})\textrm{ is free (over ${\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}$)}.

Note that
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
under our running hypotheses (cf. Lemma
5.19
), and it is of rank 1.

Since
T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger}
is self-dual, the cup-product pairing induces a perfect pairing on
H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
.
This pairing induces a perfect pairing

(5.19)

H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{0}/\mathscr{F}^{2})\times H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}.

To lighten our notation, let us denote by
L
the image of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
inside
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{0}/\mathscr{F}^{2})
, and write
L^{\rm sat}
for its saturation. We first prove that

(5.20)

L^{\rm sat}{}^{\perp}=L^{\perp}=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}}).

Note that the first equality in (
5.20
) is clear since the pairing (
5.19
) takes values in the integral domain
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
. Moreover, since both
L
and
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
are of rank 1 and
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
is torsion-free, we have

L^{\perp}=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\,\Longleftrightarrow\,L^{\perp}\supset H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\,\Longleftrightarrow\,L\,\perp\,H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\,.

As a result, once again relying on the fact that the pairing (
5.19
) takes values in the integral domain
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
, the sought after equality (
5.20
) follows once we check that

(5.21)

L\otimes\mathcal{Q}\quad\perp\quad H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\,,

where
\mathcal{Q}
is the field of fractions of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
.

As the complex
\mathbf{R}\Gamma(G_{\ell},T^{\dagger}_{{\underline{\Pi}}})\otimes\mathcal{Q}
is acyclic for each prime
\ell\neq p
, it follows from Nekovář’s duality
[
Nek06
, Proposition 6.7.7]
that the cup-product induces an isomorphism

\Hom_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q})[-3]\cong\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\,.

We, therefore, have an exact triangle

\displaystyle\Hom_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q})[-3]\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\mathbf{R}\Gamma(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}\,,

which yields the exact sequence

H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\otimes\mathcal{Q}=\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\mathscr{F}^{2})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})\otimes\mathcal{Q}\longrightarrow\Hom_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}^{1}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\otimes\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{Q}).

This precisely means (
5.21
) holds, and the proof of our claim (
5.20
) is complete.

Since
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{0}/\mathscr{F}^{2})/L^{\rm sat}
is torsion-free, Lemma
5.26
shows that

\Hom_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}(H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{0}/\mathscr{F}^{2}),{\mathcal{R}}_{3})\longrightarrow\Hom_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}(L^{\rm sat},{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})

is surjective. We therefore obtain an
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
-isomorphism

H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Hom_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}(L^{\rm sat},{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}).

This fact shows that
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
is reflexive, and hence it is free by Lemma
5.25
; which, in turn, implies that the inclusion morphism
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\to H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})
admits a section. Therefore, the inclusion map
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\to H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})

also admits a section, and hence, we have an
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
-isomorphism

(5.22)

\displaystyle H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})\cong H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\oplus H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\,.

Since both
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})
and
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
are reflexive, the quotient
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
is also reflexive. We infer from Lemma
5.25
that

H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
is free. In particular,
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})
is free.
Using again Nekovář’s duality, we obtain
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})^{\perp}=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})
, and hence an isomorphism

H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Hom_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}(H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}),{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}).

This concludes our proof that
H^{1}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})
is free, as required.

The same argument shows that
H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})/H^{1}_{M}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
is free, and our proof is complete.
∎

5.2.6.

By Lemma
5.27
, there is a surjection of
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-modules
H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi^{*}_{3,2}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})\xrightarrow{\ell_{\underline{\Pi}}}R_{3}^{2}
such that
\ker(\ell_{\underline{\Pi}})=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})
.
We put

\psi_{i}:=\mathrm{pr}_{i}\circ\ell_{\underline{\Pi}}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\quad(i=1,2)\,\,\,\textrm{ and }\,\,\,\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}:=\psi_{1}\wedge\psi_{2}.

Let
\psi_{3},\psi_{4}\in{\rm Hom}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}(H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}),{\mathcal{R}}_{3})
be such that

\psi_{3}\oplus\psi_{4}\oplus\ell_{\underline{\Pi}}\,\colon\,H^{1}(G_{p},\varpi_{3,2}^{*}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})\,\longrightarrow\,{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{4}

is an isomorphism.

Theorem 5.29
.

Assume that Hypotheses
5.2.1
hold. Then,

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\times(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p})(\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\,.

Proof.

The asserted factorisation follows from the following chain of equalities:

\displaystyle\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))

\displaystyle=(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)})(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))\times(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p})(\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})).

Here, the first equality is Proposition
5.23
, and the final equality holds thanks to definitions.
∎

5.2.7.
Basis of
H^{1}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})

For each
i\in\{0,1,2,3\}
, let us choose basis
\{f_{0},\cdots,f_{i}\}\subset H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{3-i})
so that

\langle f_{i},f_{3-j}\rangle=(-1)^{i}\delta_{ij},

where
\delta_{ij}
is the Kronecker delta function.

5.2.8.
Basis of
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})

By Lemma
5.27
, the
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-module
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
is free of rank 2.
Moreover, the isomorphism (
5.22
) shows that

H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})/(H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})\otimes{\mathcal{R}}_{3})
is free of rank 1. One can therefore take a basis of
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})
of the form

\{\varphi_{2}:=a_{1}f_{0}+b_{1}f_{1}+c_{1}f_{2}+d_{1}f_{3}\,,\,\,\varphi_{1}:=c_{2}f_{0}+d_{2}f_{1}\}\,.

As we have seen in the proof of Lemma
5.27
,
the quotient
H^{1}(G_{p},\mathscr{F}^{2})/H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
is free, that is,

{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{2}/(c_{2},d_{2}){\mathcal{R}}_{3}
is free.
As a result, since
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
is a local ring,

•

c_{2}
or
d_{2}
is a unit.

Moreover, since
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})^{\perp}
, we have

0=\langle\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\rangle=c_{2}d_{1}-d_{2}c_{1}.

Putting all this together, we conclude that there is an element
r\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
such that

\begin{pmatrix}c_{1}\\
d_{1}\end{pmatrix}=r\begin{pmatrix}c_{2}\\
d_{2}\end{pmatrix}\,.

We thus obtain the following result.

Lemma 5.30
.

Under Hypotheses
5.2.1
, the
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-module
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})
has a basis of the form

\{\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}:=a_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{0}+b_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{1}+r_{{\underline{\Pi}}}c_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{2}+r_{{\underline{\Pi}}}d_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{3}\,,\,\,\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}:=c_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{0}+d_{{\underline{\Pi}}}f_{1}\}\,,

where at least one of
c_{{\underline{\Pi}}}
and
d_{{\underline{\Pi}}}
is a unit.

5.2.9.
Basis of
H^{1}_{M}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})

Applying a similar argument as in the previous section, we obtain the following:

Lemma 5.31
.

Under Hypotheses
5.2.1
, the
{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
-module
H^{1}_{M}(G_{p},T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})
has a basis of the form

\{\varphi_{2,M}:=a_{M}f_{0}+b_{M}f_{1}+r_{M}c_{M}f_{2}+r_{M}d_{M}f_{3}\,,\,\,\varphi_{1,M}:=c_{M}f_{0}+d_{M}f_{1}\}\,,

where at least one of
c_{M}
and
d_{M}
is a unit.

5.2.10.
An explicit version of Theorem
5.29

Since
\mathrm{res}_{p}(\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))\subset\bigwedge^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
, by Lemma
5.30
, there is an element
x_{\underline{\Pi}}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
such that

\mathrm{res}_{p}(\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{\underline{\Pi}}\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\wedge\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}.

Similarly, by Lemma
5.31
, there is an element
x_{M}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
such that

\mathrm{res}_{p}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}\varphi_{1,M}\wedge\varphi_{2,M}.

Proposition 5.32
.

In the setting of Theorem
5.29
, we have

\displaystyle\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{\underline{\Pi}}x_{M}r_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})^{2}\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}\,\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}r_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})^{2}\,,

\displaystyle(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p})\,\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{\underline{\Pi}}\,.

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle\varphi_{1,M}\wedge\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\wedge\varphi_{2,M}\wedge\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}=-r_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})^{2}f_{0}\wedge f_{1}\wedge f_{2}\wedge f_{3},

the first assertion in our proposition follows from Proposition
5.23
:

\displaystyle\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\mathrm{Log}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}^{(4)}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})\otimes\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset}))={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{\underline{\Pi}}x_{M}r_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})^{2}.

Next, let us prove that

\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}\,\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})^{2}.

Since
H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})^{\perp}=H^{1}_{\underline{\Pi}}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
, we have

\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}(\,-\,)=\langle-,\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\wedge\langle-,\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle.

As
\mathrm{res}_{p}(\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a}))={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}\varphi_{1,M}\wedge\varphi_{2,M}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Log}_{/{\underline{\Pi}}}^{(2)}\,\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\rm tr}^{*}\Delta_{a})={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}\times\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle&\langle\varphi_{2,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\\
\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle&\langle\varphi_{2,M},\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\end{pmatrix}.

Since
\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle=0
, we have

\displaystyle\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle&\langle\varphi_{2,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\\
\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle&\langle\varphi_{2,M},\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=-\langle\varphi_{2,M},\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle\times\langle\varphi_{1,M},\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=-r_{M}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}})\times r_{\underline{\Pi}}(c_{M}d_{\underline{\Pi}}-d_{M}c_{\underline{\Pi}}).

This concludes the proof of the second equality.

Let us next show that
(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p})\,\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{\underline{\Pi}}
.
Note that there exist
s_{3},s_{4}\in H^{1}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
such that
\psi_{i}=\langle-,s_{i}\rangle
. Then
\{s_{1}:=\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}},s_{2}:=\varphi_{2,{\underline{\Pi}}},s_{3},s_{4}\}
is a basis of
H^{1}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
, and

\det(\langle s_{i},s_{j}\rangle)\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{\times}

since the local Tate-duality pairing on
H^{1}(G_{p},T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}})
is perfect. Since
\langle s_{i},s_{j}\rangle=0
for
i,j\in\{1,2\}
, it follows that

\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle s_{1},s_{3}\rangle&\langle s_{2},s_{3}\rangle\\
\langle s_{1},s_{4}\rangle&\langle s_{2},s_{4}\rangle\end{pmatrix}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{3}^{\times}.

This concludes the proof that

(\psi_{3}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p}\wedge\psi_{4}\circ\mathrm{res}_{p})\,\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\emptyset})={\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}x_{\underline{\Pi}}\times\det\begin{pmatrix}\langle s_{1},s_{3}\rangle&\langle s_{2},s_{3}\rangle\\
\langle s_{1},s_{4}\rangle&\langle s_{2},s_{4}\rangle\end{pmatrix}={\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}x_{\underline{\Pi}}\,,

as required.
∎

5.2.11.
Proof of Theorem
5.13

Note that we have

\displaystyle\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{b})={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}r_{M}d_{M}\varphi_{1,M},\qquad\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})={\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}x_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{\underline{\Pi}}d_{\underline{\Pi}}\varphi_{1,{\underline{\Pi}}}

by definition. As a result,

\displaystyle\Log_{c}\,\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{b})\,\times\Log_{c}\,\delta(T^{\dagger}_{\underline{\Pi}},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})={\mathcal{R}}_{3}x_{M}x_{\underline{\Pi}}r_{M}r_{\underline{\Pi}}d_{\underline{\Pi}}^{2}d_{M}^{2}.

The proof of Theorem
5.13
follows from this, combined with Proposition
5.32
.

6.
Endoscopic cases

In this section, we discuss the endoscopic cases where the
\GSp_{4}
-representation
\Pi
(and families of such) arises from a smaller group. We remark that the formulation of our factorisation conjectures dwells on the interpolation of Eichler–Shimura isomorphisms (cf.
[
LZ21a
,
DRW25b
]
). The critical points (in the sense of Bellaïche–Chenevier, which contains the set of “endoscopic points” that we describe below) over the
\GSp_{4}
-eigenvariety are excluded from the locus of interpolation. As a result, the factorisation results in this particular case assume a slightly different shape, in that the graded piece of the local Galois representation that one trivialises is different from the generic scenario. Our Corollaries
6.12
and
6.14
below reflect this fact; see also the discussion in Remark
6.17
.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
\Pi
be a cuspidal automorphic representation of
\GSp_{4}
of weight
(k_{1},k_{2})
, with
k_{1}\geq k_{2}\geq 2
, and trivial central character.

(a)

We say that
\Pi
is of Yoshida type if there is a pair
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2})
of cuspidal automorphic representations of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
, corresponding to two elliptic modular newforms of weights
t_{1}=k_{1}+k_{2}-2
and
t_{2}=k_{1}-k_{2}+2
, such that for all but finitely many places
v
we have

L(s,\Pi_{v})=L(s,\pi_{1,v})L(s,\pi_{2,v}).

(b)

We say that
\Pi
is of Saito–Kurokawa type if
k_{1}=k_{2}
and there exists a cuspidal automorphic representation
\pi
of
\mathrm{GL}_{2}
attached to some holomorphic newform of weight
2k_{1}-2
such that for all but finitely many places
v
we have

L(s,\Pi_{v})=L(s,\pi_{v})\zeta\left(s-\tfrac{1}{2}\right)\zeta\left(s+\tfrac{1}{2}\right).

These definitions may be extended to the case where the central character is non-trivial, but considering the appropriate twists but we shall limit our discussion to the scenario where it is trivial.

6.1.
Yoshida lifts

Let us put
19
19

19

We implicitly consider
T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}
as the Galois representation associated to a family
{\underline{\Pi}}
of Yoshida lifts. Such families of Siegel modular forms were recently constructed by M-L. Hsieh and Z. Liu. The ad hoc definition of the Galois representation above suffices for our purposes in this subsection.

T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}=(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\,\oplus\,(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})
, where
{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}
is a Hida family,
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}
is the irreducible component of the universal Hecke algebra determined by
{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}
, and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}:={\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}\,\widehat{\otimes}\,{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}
.

Recall our running assumption that the global root number of the members of
{\underline{\Pi}}
(at the central critical point) equals
-1
. That means one of the families
{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}
has global root number
-1
, and the other
+1
; so let us assume that

(6.1)

\varepsilon(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})=1=-\varepsilon(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})\,.

6.1.1.

The Galois representation
T_{?}^{\dagger}
(where
?=3,4
) admits the following decomposition:

(6.2)

T_{?}^{\dagger}\simeq\underbrace{\left(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\underline{\sigma}}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{*}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{T_{?,1}^{\dagger}}\quad\oplus\quad\underbrace{\left(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\underline{\sigma}}\widehat{\otimes}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{*}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{T_{?,2}^{\dagger}}\,.

When
?=3
, each summand above further decomposes as

(6.3)

T_{3}^{\dagger}\simeq\underbrace{\left((T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}T_{\underline{\sigma}})\oplus T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{T_{3,1}^{\dagger}}\quad\oplus\quad\underbrace{\left((T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}T_{\underline{\sigma}})\oplus T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{T_{3,2}^{\dagger}}\,.

Moreover, in view of our discussion in §
2.5.1
, we have:

-

Region (a) corresponds to the
\underline{\sigma}^{c}
-dominant region for both triple products
T_{4,?}^{\dagger}
(
?=1,2
).

-

Region (b) corresponds to the
\underline{\sigma}^{c}
-dominant region for
T_{4,2}^{\dagger}
and balanced region for
T_{4,1}^{\dagger}
.

-

Region (c) corresponds to the balanced region for both.

Based on this, (
6.2
), and (
6.4
), we set
L_{p}^{(a)}(T_{?}^{\dagger}):=L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{?,1}^{\dagger})\times L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{?,2}^{\dagger})
.

6.1.2.

We also have the decomposition

(6.4)

M_{3}^{\dagger}\simeq\underbrace{\left(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}T_{\underline{\sigma}}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}}\quad\oplus\quad\underbrace{\left(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes{\rm ad}^{0}T_{\underline{\sigma}}\right)\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}_{M_{3,2}^{\dagger}}.

6.1.3.

Recall that we work under the assumption that the global root number of the specialisations of
T_{4}^{\dagger}
in region (b) is
-1
, and equivalently, it is
+1
for those in region (c):

\displaystyle 1=-\varepsilon^{(b)}(T_{4}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\,=\varepsilon^{(c)}(T_{4}^{\dagger})=\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(T_{4,1}^{\dagger})\,\cdot\,\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(T_{4,2}^{\dagger})\,=\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(M_{3,1})\,\varepsilon(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}})\,\cdot\,\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2})\,\varepsilon(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}})\,.

That means
T_{4,1}^{\dagger}
and
T_{4,2}^{\dagger}
have the same sign in the balanced region. We assume that this sign is
-1
, so that we have

-\varepsilon^{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}(M_{3,1})=\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(M_{3,1})=-1=-\varepsilon^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2})=\varepsilon^{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}(M_{3,2})\,.

Remark 6.2
.

Unlike in the non-endoscopic cases, classical specialisations of
M_{3}^{\dagger}
in the region (a) are critical in the sense of Deligne: As we have noted above, these correspond to
\underline{\sigma}^{c}
-dominant conditions on both of the
\mathrm{GL}_{2}\times\mathrm{GL}_{3}
factors. Note that “
\underline{\sigma}^{c}
-dominant” is synonymous with “balanced” for these factors.

Conjecture 6.3
.

Under the current assumptions, the following factorisation formula holds:

(6.5)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} L_{p}^{(a)}(T_{3}^{\dagger})^{2}&:=L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{3,1}^{\dagger})^{2}\times L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{3,2}^{\dagger})^{2}\\
&\,=\left(\Log_{\omega_{M_{3,1}}}(\Delta_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}})^{2}\times L_{p}({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1})\right)\times\left(L_{p}^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2}^{\dagger})\times\Log_{\omega_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}(\Delta_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})^{2}\right)\\
&\,=\underbrace{\left(\Log_{\omega_{M_{3,1}}}(\Delta_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}})^{2}\times L_{p}^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2}^{\dagger})\right)}_{\Log_{\omega_{M_{3}}}(\Delta_{M_{3}^{\dagger}}^{(c)})^{2}}\times\underbrace{\left(L_{p}({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1})\times\Log_{\omega_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}(\Delta_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})^{2}\right)}_{\Log_{\omega_{\underline{\Pi}}}(\Delta_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger})^{2}}\,.\end{aligned}

up to multiplication by an element of
{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}[\frac{1}{p}]^{\times}
that specialises to an explicit algebraic number at all classical specialisations of
({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1},{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2})
. Here:

•

The trivialisation
\Log_{\omega_{M_{3,1}}}
is defined at the start of §
6.2.5
below.

•

\Delta_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},M_{3,1}^{\dagger})
is the conjectural family of twisted diagonal cycles (cf.
[
HY23
]
and the forthcoming work of Chida–Hsieh–Prasanna), whereas
\Delta_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\in H^{1}(\mathbb{Q},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})
is the family of diagonal cycles of Darmon–Rotger and Bertolini–Seveso–Venerucci.

•

L_{p}({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1})
is the restricion of the Mazur–Kitagawa
p
-adic
L
-function to the central critical line, whereas
L_{p}^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2}^{\dagger})
is the same for the conjectural
p
-adic
L
-function associated to the family
M_{3,2}^{\dagger}
(cf.
[
BC25
]
, Conjecture 2.4).

•

\Delta_{M_{3}^{\dagger}}^{(c)}:=L_{p}^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2}^{\dagger})\cdot\Delta_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}}
and
\Delta_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}:=L_{p}({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1})\cdot\Delta_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}
.

In (
6.5
), assuming the truth of the Gross--Kudla conjecture
20
20

20

In its more general form as
[
BCPdVP25
, Conjecture 5.1]
.
and the “non-anomality condition” (
NA)
), the factorisation

L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{3,2}^{\dagger})^{2}\,=\,L_{p}^{\rm bal}(M_{3,2}^{\dagger})\times\Log(\Delta_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})^{2}\pmod{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\times}}

is proved in

]
and its sequel
[

]
. The remaining part of the conjectured equality (

) is the assertion that

(6.6)

L_{p}^{(\underline{\sigma}^{c})}(T_{3,1}^{\dagger})^{2}\,\dot{=}\,\Log(\Delta_{M_{3,1}^{\dagger}})^{2}\times L_{p}({\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1})\,,

and it supplements the results of

would allow one to give an unconditional proof of Conjecture

when the family

We refer the reader to Theorem

for its algebraic counterpart in terms of modules of leading terms.

6.2.
The algebraic counterpart of Conjecture
6.3

As we have remarked at the start of §
6
, Theorem
5.13
needs to be modified in the endoscopic cases. In this subsection, we explain why indeed this is necessary (cf. Corollary
6.12
, Corollary
6.14
and Remark
6.17
) and prove (in Corollary
6.18
) the appropriate variant of Theorem
5.13
in the setting of §
6.1
(which is the algebraic counterpart of Conjecture
6.3
).

6.2.1.
Filtration on
T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}

We consider the filtration
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}
on
T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}=(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\,\oplus\,(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})
and determine how it propagates to each summand.
Since
(\mathscr{F}^{2})^{\perp}=\mathscr{F}^{2}
, we have

\mathscr{F}^{2}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}=F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}\quad\oplus\quad F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}\,.

Here
F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}
denotes the rank one
G_{p}
-submodule of
T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}
, whose existence is guaranteed by the
p
-ordinarity.
Moreover, the fact that
\mathscr{F}^{3}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}
has rank one implies that it must be equal to
F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}
for some
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Furthermore, using the relation
(\mathscr{F}^{3})^{\perp}=\mathscr{F}^{1}
, it follows that

\mathscr{F}^{1}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}=\begin{cases}F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}\quad\oplus\quad T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}&\qquad\textrm{if}\quad i=1,\\
T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}\quad\oplus\quad F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}&\qquad\textrm{if}\quad i=2.\end{cases}

To summarize, we have two possible candidates for the filtration
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}
on
T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}
, each of which is determined by the choice of
\mathscr{F}^{3}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}
:

Definition 6.4
.

For each integer
i\in\{1,2\}
, we call
the filtration
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{i}
on
T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}
satisfying
\mathscr{F}^{3}T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger}=F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}
an
f_{i}
-dominant filtration.

6.2.2.

In what follows, for any
{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}
-module (or a complex of
{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}
-modules)
M
, let us write
M_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
(resp.,
M_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}
) in place of
M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}
(resp. of
M\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}
) to ease our notation. The following three propositions follow immediately from the definition of
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{i}
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{1}
.

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}},}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{1}})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{F^{+}})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}},}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{F^{+}})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{F^{+}})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}},}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{3}})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{F^{+}})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}},}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{4}})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{0})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\,.}

In the case of
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{2}
, the roles of
{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}
and
{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}
are reversed.

By definition, we have a canonical homomorphism

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\longrightarrow{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}).

It therefore becomes possible to compare
\delta(T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})
with
\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\otimes\,\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
.

Corollary 6.6
.

We have

(6.8)

\delta(T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})=\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\,\otimes\,\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\,.

Proof.

If
\delta(T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})=0
, then at least one of
\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
or
\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
must vanish.
Thus, we may assume that all three are nonzero.
Then Theorem
4.3
(ii) and Proposition
6.5
shows that

\displaystyle\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\left.{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})\middle/\delta(T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right.\right)

\displaystyle=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})\right)

\displaystyle=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\times\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\mathbf{R}^{2}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})\right)_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}

\displaystyle=\Char_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\left(\left.\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\,\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\middle/\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\otimes\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\right.\right).

Since both
{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\Pi}}}}\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{0}})
and
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\,\otimes\,\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
are free of rank
1
and the homomorphism (
6.7
) is an isomorphism, we obtain the desired equality
\delta(T_{\underline{\Pi}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})=\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}\otimes\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
.
∎

Recall that we have two Greenberg local conditions
\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}
and
\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}
on
T_{3,i}^{\dagger}
, as defined in
[
BS25
, Example 4.1]
. These are referred to as the
{\underline{\sigma}}
-dominant Greenberg local condition and the balanced Greenberg local condition, respectively. They are defined via the following choices of submodules of
T_{3,i}^{\dagger}
:

•

The
{\underline{\sigma}}
-dominant condition
\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}
is defined by the submodule

F_{\underline{\sigma}}^{+}T_{3,i}^{\dagger}:=(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\dagger}\,\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\sigma}}}\,T_{\underline{\sigma}^{c}}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow T_{3,i}^{\dagger}.

•

The balanced condition
\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}
is defined by the sum of submodules

\displaystyle F_{\rm bal}^{+}T_{3,i}^{\dagger}:=(F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\,\widehat{\otimes}\,F^{+}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\dagger}\otimes T_{\underline{\sigma}^{c}}^{\dagger}+(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\,

\displaystyle\widehat{\otimes}F^{+}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\dagger}\otimes F^{+}T_{\underline{\sigma}^{c}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle+(F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\otimes_{{\mathcal{R}}_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}}{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}})\widehat{\otimes}T_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\dagger}\otimes F^{+}T_{\underline{\sigma}^{c}}^{\dagger}\hookrightarrow T_{3,i}^{\dagger}\,.

Moreover, using the homomorphism
\mathrm{tr}^{*}\colon T_{3,i}\longrightarrow M_{3,i}
, we obtain two induced Greenberg local conditions on
M_{3,i}
, denoted by
\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}
and
\mathrm{tr}^{*}\Delta_{\mathrm{bal}}
.

Proposition 6.7
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{1}
.

\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{a})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}),}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{b})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}),}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{c})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal}),}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{b}^{\perp})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal})}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\perp})\,.}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{a}^{\perp})}
\textstyle{=}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\perp})}
\textstyle{\oplus}
\textstyle{\mathbf{R}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}^{\perp})\,.}

In the case of
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{2}
, the roles of
M_{3,1}^{\dagger}
and
M_{3,2}^{\dagger}
are reversed.

Arguing in the same way as in the proof of Corollary
6.6
, we obtain the following:

Corollary 6.8
.

We have

(6.9)

\delta(M_{3}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{a})=\delta(M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})\otimes\delta(M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}}).

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
\{f_{0},f_{1},f_{2},f_{3}\}
be the basis of
H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger})
given as in §
5.2.7
.

If
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{1}
, then
we have
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}f_{0}=H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}f_{1}=H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})
. Moreover,
f_{2}\in H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})
and
f_{3}\in H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})
.

If
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{2}
, then we have
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}f_{0}=H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}f_{1}=H^{1}(G_{p},F^{+}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})
. Moreover,
f_{2}\in H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})
and
f_{3}\in H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})
.

Corollary 6.10
.

In the setting of Proposition
6.9
, if
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{1}
, then
c_{\underline{\Pi}}=0
and
d_{\underline{\Pi}}
is a unit. If
\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}=\mathscr{F}^{\bullet}_{2}
, then
c_{\underline{\Pi}}
is unit and
d_{\underline{\Pi}}=0
.

Proof.

The saturation of the image of
\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\Pi}}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\mathscr{F}^{2}})
equals
H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\underline{\Pi}}}
. This corollary therefore follows from Proposition
6.9
.
∎

Proof of Theorem

.

\delta(T_{3}^{\dagger},\Delta_{a})=\Log^{(4)}_{\mathscr{F}^{0}}(\delta(M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})\otimes\delta(M_{3,2}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})\otimes\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\otimes\delta(T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{2}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}})\,,

Since we have the decomposition

Here,

\displaystyle\Log^{(2)}_{i}\colon\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,i}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})

\displaystyle\otimes\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}(\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,i}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})\oplus\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger},\Delta_{\emptyset})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow{\bigcap}^{2}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{i}}^{\dagger})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\cong{\mathcal{R}}_{3}.

We next define the morphism

\Log_{F^{-}}\,\colon\,H^{1}(G_{p},T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\longrightarrow H^{1}(G_{p},F^{-}T_{{\underline{\mathbf{f}}}_{1}}^{\dagger})_{{\mathcal{R}}_{3}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{R}}_{3}\,.

Since

by the assumption (

-

M

3
,
1

)
), it follows from Theorem

that

\delta(M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})\in\mathbf{R}^{1}\Gamma_{\fin}(G_{\mathbb{Q},\Sigma},M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\rm bal}),

and hence

\Log_{F^{-}}(\mathrm{res}_{p}(\delta(M_{3,1}^{\dagger},{\mathrm{tr}}^{*}\Delta_{\underline{\sigma}})))=0.

The definition of
\Log^{(2)}_{1}
, together with Theorem
